- **[Householder Motion Embedding: Noise Modulation for Motion-Controllable Video Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07294)**  `arXiv:2611.07294`  
  _Tao Jun Lin, Jinhong Ni, Ruyi Zha, Yifu Wang, Xibin Song, Pan Ji, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Diffusion models for video generation have achieved remarkable success, yet precise motion control under explicit user guidance remains challenging. Recent noise-warping methods introduce temporal correlations by warping the noise according to the user-provided motion signal. However, standard spatial warping generally disrupts the noise's i.i.d. Gaussian structure, requiring distribution correction, such as Jacobian-based correction, but at a substantial computational overhead. We propose Householder Motion Embedding (HME), which encodes the motion signal into the noise through a sequence of Householder reflections that together form an orthogonal transformation applied to each location's channel vector. HME exactly preserves the spatial Gaussian structure within each frame while introducing cross-frame correlations that promote temporally consistent video. HME is agnostic to the network architecture and integrates directly into standard video diffusion fine-tuning pipelines. Experiments on different scenarios show that HME matches or outperforms the state of the art, while achieving a 2.7x speedup over noise warping. These results demonstrate that channel-space Householder transformations offer a principled and efficient alternative to noise warping for motion-controllable video generation.
  </details>

- **[MammoSeek: A Mammography Agent with selective zooming for grounded reports](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07188)**  `arXiv:2611.07188`  
  _Haoyan Ding, Qian Wang, Lixia Wang, Debiao Li_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Radiologists read a screening mammogram by first forming a global impression of the four views and then selectively magnifying suspicious regions before assigning a BI-RADS assessment. MLLMs, in contrast, see the whole image at a fixed, reduced resolution, which makes sub-millimeter calcifications invisible, and they perform close to chance on mammography. Therefore, we present MammoSeek, a mammography agent that reproduces this reading process. After forming an impression of each breast, the agent performs selective zooming: it decides by itself which detector candidates deserve a closer look and inspects only those on full-resolution views. Guided by a BI-RADS assessment skill, it then assigns a category and generates a localized, lexicon-structured report for each breast; its assessment is combined with image-model scores in a decision layer to decide recall. On an internal (EMBED) and an external (VinDr-Mammo) dataset, \ours reaches exam-level AUCs of 0.824 and 0.818, far above other MLLMs, mammography agent and CNN classifiers. Selective zooming also outperforms both no zooming and forced zooming in localization, structured-report F1 and recall decisions at low cost.
  </details>

- **[VINO: Revisiting 3D Representation Learning with Volume Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07176)**  `arXiv:2611.07176`  
  _Adrian Kruse, Kadir Yilmaz, Daan de Geus, Francis Engelmann, Bastian Leibe_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Self-supervised learning has given rise to vision foundation models that learn general-purpose image representations transferable across downstream tasks, yet representation learning for 3D scenes has not achieved comparable success. Motivated by this, we identify key limitations of current 3D representation learning approaches and revisit their core design choices. We first replace the commonly used hierarchical PTv3 backbone with the Volume Transformer, a ViT-like architecture for 3D scenes. We then redesign two core objectives of modern approaches built around student-teacher networks: matching representations across views and predicting masked content from visible context. For the first objective, we separate the roles of teacher and student: the teacher provides clean, consistent targets while the student learns invariance to stronger perturbations. For the second objective, we prevent the student from accessing the masked geometry and instead require it to infer missing representations from visible context only. Finally, we redesign 2D-to-3D knowledge distillation, replacing direct regression of view-dependent image features with view-consistent supervision. Together, these changes form VINO, a framework that learns semantic representations of 3D scenes without labels. VINO substantially improves dense linear probing across indoor and outdoor benchmarks and transfers strongly to out-of-domain settings.
  </details>

- **[Perceptual Distortion for Overfitted Video Coding: Sliced Matching in Space, Error Alignment in Time](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07148)**  `arXiv:2611.07148`  
  _Tianyi Yang, Ge Gao, Fan Zhang, David Bull_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Recent works in neural video coding have increasingly been trading off perceptual quality against reconstruction fidelity at a fixed bitrate. Wasserstein Distortion (WD) offers a principled approach to balancing these objectives by relaxing spatial correspondences in feature matching, enabling lightweight overfitted image codecs to compete with computationally intensive generative decoders. Extending vanilla WD to video, however, presents two challenges: its independent Gaussian approximation limits the expressiveness of feature distribution matching, while unconstrained texture resampling across frames introduces temporal inconsistencies. We address these challenges by distinguishing between the correspondences that can be relaxed and those that must be preserved. Spatially, we introduce a local sliced Wasserstein distortion (SWD) that matches empirical feature distributions through one-dimensional optimal transport, capturing cross-channel dependencies without enforcing exact spatial alignment. Temporally, we employ a novel flow residual that constrains coding errors along motion trajectories, preserving temporal consistency without imposing additional reconstruction fidelity constraints. Evaluated on UVG and HEVC Class B with the overfitted codec COOL-CHIC, our framework achieves BD-rate reductions of 9.2% in DISTS and 3.0% in LPIPS relative to frame-wise WD, averaged over test sequences, while lowering tOF by 10.2% at matched rate.
  </details>

- **[A Proxy-Based Method for Measuring Racial Disparities in~Text-to-Image~Generators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07132)**  `arXiv:2611.07132`  
  _Nia Mucher, Ngan Pham, Jason Chin, Aaron Brown, David Evans_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  We present an automated method for auditing text-to-image generators for representational bias against ground-truth demographic benchmarks. Our approach adapts the racial proxy variables used in U.S. antidiscrimination law to provide a fine-grained comparative analysis of variations in racial representation in generated images. We classify each generated image by race and then compare the model's distribution to publicly available demographic benchmark data to identify patterns of over- and under-representation. We also measure skin tone using CIELAB values and calculate cosine similarity amongst groups of images in order to capture biases that don't show up in demographic comparisons such as differences in image quality across races and skin tones. We report on experiments testing three models (Imagen 4 Fast, Stable Diffusion 3.5 Large Turbo, and Flux Schnell) using three categories of proxies (geographic, occupational, and surname) and 50 variants within each category. We find notable variation across the models as well as by proxy category. For example, Imagen 4 Fast overrepresents white classified images more than the other models tested and produces images with lighter, more homogeneous skin tones. Imagen 4 Fast also had the largest representational disparity for Black demographics among surname and geographical prompts. Stable Diffusion 3.5 Large Turbo produces results that overall diverge most from census data but yield the most diverse range of skin tones. Our method yields a generalizable and legally-grounded auditing tool that surfaces detailed information about model outputs without relying on implicit normative definitions of fairness.
  </details>

- **[Wide View Synthesis from Overlapping Perspective Cameras in Autonomous Driving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07081)**  `arXiv:2611.07081`  
  _Kaixuan Zhang, Zhipeng Xiong, Minxian Li, Mingwu Ren, Xiatian Zhu_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Multi-perspective-camera systems are widely adopted since they provide broad visual coverage for autonomous driving, motivating the synthesis of a unified wide view from spatially separated observations. In this work, we formulate wide view synthesis (WVS), which aims to synthesize a geometrically consistent wide view at a specified virtual viewpoint from multiple perspective cameras. Unlike conventional image stitching, WVS requires reasoning about 3D geometry and visibility to handle depth-dependent parallax and occlusions arising from spatially separated camera centers. While vanilla 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) enables high-fidelity view synthesis, its reliance on per-scene optimization limits its applicability to driving scenes. Furthermore, WVS introduces additional challenges, *e.g.*, peripheral stretching under perspective projection and photometric inconsistencies across cameras. To address these challenges, we propose **Wide-View Gaussian Splatting (WV-GS)**, a feed-forward Gaussian-splatting framework that synthesizes wide views without per-scene optimization. Specifically, we introduce Piecewise Perspective Rendering (PPR) to mitigate peripheral stretching through local perspective projections sharing a common camera center, and Spatial Affine Photometric Modeling (SAPM) to account for camera-dependent photometric variations during training. We further introduce **WideDrive**, a synthetic benchmark providing dense ground-truth wide views and adapt a LiDAR-projected sparse-reference protocol to real-world datasets. Extensive experiments on multiple datasets demonstrate the effectiveness of WV-GS for WVS. Dataset and code will be publicly released.
  </details>

- **[SD-LoRA: Training-Time Structural Distillation into LoRA for Few-Shot Vision-Language Adaptation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07063)**  `arXiv:2611.07063`  
  _Mohammed Rahman Sherif Khan Mohammad, Ardhendu Behera, Sandip Pradhan, Swagat Kumar, Yonghuai Liu_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Few-shot adaptation of vision-language models must balance two competing goals: learning fine-grained visual distinctions from very limited supervision and preserving the efficiency that makes frozen CLIP-like models attractive for deployment. Existing lightweight adaptation methods, including prompt tuning, LoRA, and cache-based adapters, typically supervise global image embeddings and therefore underuse token-level structure. Conversely, structure-aware methods exploit local evidence but often introduce additional inference-time computation. We propose SD-LoRA, a structural distillation framework that uses token-level graph reasoning only during training and transfers its effects into a lightweight LoRA-adapted CLIP model. During training, a heterogeneous graph teacher models patch-patch, global-local, and visual-text relations over high-resolution ViT tokens. Rather than retaining this graph at test time, SD-LoRA transfers its structural bias into the shared CLIP-LoRA backbone through implicit distillation and Prototype Predictive Alignment, which aligns graph-conditioned representations with support-derived class prototypes. At inference, the graph teacher is discarded; prediction requires only a single CLIP-LoRA forward pass and a cache-based classifier. Across 11 few-shot benchmarks, SD-LoRA improves over a strong CLIP-LoRA baseline by 2.4/1.8/3.0/2.4/2.0 percentage points in the 1/2/4/8/16-shot settings, respectively, without additional graph computation at test time. Results on base-to-novel generalization, cross-dataset transfer, out-of-distribution robustness, and calibration further show that training-time structural supervision improves both accuracy and transfer reliability. Code is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[Segment What It Can Draw: Querying a Text-to-Image Generator with Its Own Images for Skin Lesion Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07061)**  `arXiv:2611.07061`  
  _Hongyu Cao, Guangfu Guo, Jinghan Zhang, Dakshak Keerthi Chandra, Ying Zhang, Kunpeng Liu_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Skin lesion segmentation without annotated masks requires the target to be specified through other cues. Existing approaches rely on dermoscopy-specific rules, annotation-derived model priors, or user-selected regions. A general text-to-image diffusion transformer offers a text interface to the target, but its concept-word attention is unreliable on dermoscopy images: in the model's attention-output space, the word-derived skin-to-lesion direction is poorly aligned with the direction separating real skin from real lesions. We show that the direction derived from generated healthy-skin and lesion images is substantially better aligned. Based on this finding, we propose GENIQ, a training-free method that constructs initial skin and lesion prototypes from generated images and refines them using the most skin-like and lesion-like patches from unlabelled dermoscopy images. It scores test patches along the refined direction and applies a gated threshold to suppress contextual spill-over around small lesions. Without inference-time annotations or additional models, GENIQ achieves Dice scores of 0.85-0.88 on three dermoscopy test sets. It outperforms zero-shot and word-based attention methods on all three sets, and a hand-designed dermoscopy pipeline in Dice and IoU on two.
  </details>

- **[Expanding the Quality-Coverage Frontier with Competitive Flow Matching](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07055)**  `arXiv:2611.07055`  
  _Wenxuan Peng, Richard Zhang, Haitian Zheng, Jaskirat Singh, Tsai-Shien Chen, Eli Shechtman, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Flow matching (FM) generators often face a quality-coverage tradeoff. Inference-time techniques such as classifier-free guidance raise quality, but at the cost of coverage. We hypothesize this limitation stems from a basic property of the objective: an intermediate noisy image could correspond to many clean images, yet the model can only predict a single velocity, at best averaging over the multiple possibilities. To mitigate this, we introduce competition among predictions during training. We achieve this by augmenting the predictor with a randomly drawn low-dimensional conditioning code $z$. For each training example, we sample $K$ values of $z$ to produce $K$ candidate velocities that compete to explain the target, and apply the loss only to the best-matching candidate. This winner-take-all objective allows different modes to shape different predictions, rather than being averaged into a single compromise. Furthermore, this term naturally enables a new form of guidance, auxiliary conditioning scaling, which complements traditional guidance methods. Together, these two guidance mechanisms show that our method substantially improves the quality-coverage frontier. We validate on ImageNet-256, using two different testbeds, SiT and RAEv2. Given less than 3% of the original SiT pretraining compute for post-training, at the 67% Recall point, our method reduces $\mathrm{FD}_{r,6}$ from 19.22 to 5.91. On RAEv2, at the 70% Recall point, it reduces $\mathrm{FD}_{r,6}$ from 16.83 to 3.18.
  </details>

- **[Gaussians Can Subtract: Neural Dissolve Fields for Hybrid Scene Rendering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07049)**  `arXiv:2611.07049`  
  _Yiyan Wang, Haohan Liu, Tetsuo Shibuya_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Hybrid Mesh-Gaussian rendering combines compact textured surfaces with flexible 3D Gaussian primitives, offering an efficient alternative to purely Gaussian scene representations for structured environments. Existing scene-reconstruction hybrids commonly follow an additive formulation, where Gaussian residuals compensate for errors left by the mesh. However, not every Mesh-Gaussian conflict is caused by missing content: residual geometry errors, occlusion mismatch, texture-projection artifacts, and view-dependent appearance can make the same mesh content consistent in one view but inconsistent in another. We therefore formulate hybrid rendering as a multi-view inconsistency problem and present Gaussolve, a subtract-and-refill framework that selectively dissolves inconsistent mesh content before residual Gaussians refill the revealed regions. We introduce a lightweight Neural Dissolve Field, parameterized by scalar spherical-harmonic Gaussians, to learn where and from which viewing directions mesh content should be dissolved. A continuous transmittance fusion rule progressively exposes residual Gaussians behind the dissolved mesh, preserving their reconstruction gradients and avoiding hard visibility discontinuities. Experiments on structured and semi-structured scenes demonstrate higher visual fidelity with fewer Gaussian primitives and faster rendering. Project page:this https URL
  </details>

- **[TraCE: Trajectory-Aware Concepts for Explainable Video Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07009)**  `arXiv:2611.07009`  
  _Wooil Lee, Junpyo Hong, Jongseo Lee, Tuomas Oikarinen, Tsui-Wei Weng, Jinwoo Choi_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Video understanding models often rely on motion, yet their explanations can obscure which motion sources support a prediction. We identify motion-source under-specification as a limitation of existing video XAI: region-centric explanations can be source-ambiguous, while motion vocabularies restricted to single-person pose sequences are source-incomplete. We propose TraCE (Trajectory-Aware Concepts for Explainable Video Understanding), a concept-based framework that discovers source-flexible motion concepts by clustering optical-flow trajectories from training videos. These concepts represent dynamic evidence from people, objects, and interactions, while object and scene concepts account for static context. The resulting typed trajectory/object/scene interface supports class prediction for action recognition and question-conditioned concept matching for video question answering (VQA). We evaluate TraCE across five random seeds on six benchmarks: NTU RGB+D Mutual, Chiral SSv2, UCF-101, KTH, TOMATO, and STAR. Relative to non-interpretable baselines, TraCE maintains competitive task performance, with an average accuracy drop of 1.0 points for action recognition and a nominal gain of 0.4 points for VQA. Motion perturbation and concept-removal tests provide complementary evidence of directional semantic alignment and decision faithfulness, while sample-level interventions illustrate targeted model debugging. Our project page is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[Batch-Conditioned Semantic Anchors for Robust Transductive Adaptation of Vision--Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06993)**  `arXiv:2611.06993`  
  _Mohammed Rahman Sherif Khan Mohammad, Ardhendu Behera, Sandip Pradhan, Swagat Kumar, Amr Ahmed_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Transductive adaptation improves vision-language models at test time by refining predictions on an unlabeled batch, but current methods face a key trade-off. Aggressive approaches exploit batch structure but can drift when batches are sparse, have prior shift, or cover little of the label space. Conservative methods better preserve CLIP text semantics, but fixed semantic references can under-adapt even when the batch provides strong corrective evidence. We propose JPTA (Joint Prior and Transport Adaptation), a prior-aware framework that casts transductive VLM adaptation as batch-conditioned semantic reference estimation. Instead of treating CLIP text prototypes as fixed priors or freely replaceable, JPTA views them as semantic references that shift toward image-side batch structure only under calibrated evidence. It estimates batch priors and soft image-side prototypes to form transported semantic references whose impact is scaled by batch reliability. Across standard benchmarks, low effective-class regimes, all-class evaluation, and online streams, JPTA consistently outperforms TransCLIP and StatA, with the largest gains on sparse and prior-shifted batches. Anchor-drift, active-support, and absent-class-mass diagnostics show that JPTA fixes effective-class mismatch without unconstrained transductive drift, indicating that robust transductive VLM adaptation should recalibrate semantic references using reliable batch evidence rather than choose between unrestricted transduction and fixed prototypes. Code is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[Trainspotting: Learning Object-Centric 3D Reconstruction from Found Photos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06849)**  `arXiv:2611.06849`  
  _Hanyu Chen, Noah Snavely_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  State-of-the-art feedforward reconstruction models perform poorly on in-the-wild object-centric photo collections. They are trained largely on single-session object captures whose images vary in viewpoint while the object and its environment remain nearly fixed. Public photo archives contain a different form of multi-view data. They hold found photos of the same object or design: photos taken independently by many people across years, environments, cameras, and sometimes physical units. In this work, we introduce Trainspotting, a dataset of 12,068 objects and 311,886 photos from Wikimedia Commons. We reconstruct each object to recover camera poses, multi-view stereo depth, and object masks. We categorize the objects into three regimes: objects fixed at one site, objects that move between sites, and designs shared by many physical units. Evaluating recent feedforward reconstruction models on Trainspotting shows that they fail most severely in the last two regimes. Fine-tuning VGGT-$\Omega$ on Trainspotting significantly improves camera pose, depth, and 3D geometry on held-out objects. We observe similar improvements on the independent NAVI in-the-wild benchmark, which contains photos of each object in different environments. Performance on conventional benchmarks remains largely unchanged.
  </details>

- **[Open-HumanWAM: An Open Recipe for Scaling Human and Robot Experience for Embodied Foundation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06817)**  `arXiv:2611.06817`  
  _Juncheng Ma, Jiaxin Li, Jiankai Tu, Bo Liang, Guodong Lv, Yufan Deng, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Egocentric human video offers a scalable source of manipulation priors for robot learning, yet translating raw data scale into generalizable robot policies demands rigorous data curation and training recipes. We introduce Open-HumanWAM, the first fully open recipe for scaling egocentric pretraining to 50,000 hours, anchored by Open-HumanWAM-50K---a curated egocentric video corpus densely annotated with hand motion and natural language. Leveraging this corpus, we systematically investigate pure human pretraining via closed-loop simulation evaluations and uncover an empirical log-linear scaling law linking pretraining data hours to downstream robot success rates. Grounded in these findings, we develop HumanWAM-Vanilla, a world--action model pretrained exclusively on 50k hours of egocentric human video. Upon robot post-training, HumanWAM-Vanilla reaches 92.08% success on RoboTwin 2.0 Clean2Clean and 68.88% on Clean2Random, alongside a 14.17% mean success rate and a 19.00 mean progress score on RoboDojo. To push the capability frontier further, we explore joint human--robot pretraining with HumanWAM-Goal, a visual-goal-conditioned world--action model. Hybrid data scaling unlocks broad zero-shot generalization across both simulation and physical benchmarks, propelling post-trained RoboTwin 2.0 performance to new state-of-the-art levels: 92.70% on Clean2Clean and a substantial leap to 84.60% on Clean2Random. On physical robots, HumanWAM-Goal further exhibits emergent embodied in-context learning, dynamically adapting its manipulation behavior to unseen visual goals without parameter updates. To support reproducible scaling research in embodied AI, we open-source the Open-HumanWAM-50K corpus, data curation and pretraining pipelines, alongside the pretrained and post-trained HumanWAM checkpoints, inference code, and simulation evaluation suites.
  </details>

- **[EvoWorld: Towards Out-of-Sight World Evolution in Generative Video Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06748)**  `arXiv:2611.06748`  
  _Yequan Bie, Dengyang Jiang, Zhixuan Chen, Xiaozhuan Liang, Haoxuan Che, Quande Liu, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Generative video models are increasingly used as visual world simulators, yet their primary strength lies in rendering plausible pixels rather than reasoning about how a world should evolve. Coherent world evolution requires more than visual fidelity: when an event occurs, a model must infer its consequences for the entities in the world and then render the resulting state. For example, after a wild beast appears, the model should not only depict the wild beast, but also infer whether a nearby person should flee, take cover, or fight back based on the realized scene. Existing diffusion-based video models lack an explicit mechanism for maintaining world state and reasoning over such event-conditioned consequences, often producing visually plausible but behaviorally static or causally inconsistent outputs. To make this reasoning gap concrete and measurable, we propose a new problem, Out-of-Sight Causal Evolution, which requires a model to render an event, reason about its consequences for the evolving world state, and reveal a causally plausible outcome as the world continues to evolve, even out of sight. As a baseline reference solution, we introduce EvoWorld, a brain-renderer collaboration framework in which a vision-language model serves as the world brain: it observes generated segments, maintains an evolving account of the world, and reasons about plausible state transitions, while a segment-autoregressive video renderer realizes them visually. We further introduce EvoWorld-Data and EvoWorld-Bench for training and evaluating event realization, causal reactions, and revisit consistency. Experiments show that EvoWorld substantially improves event-conditioned causal evolution over recent models while preserving visual quality and consistency, establishing a reference point for reasoning-guided, autonomous world evolution beyond the current field of view.
  </details>

- **[DreamRealm: Exploring and Materializing Hallucination Worlds for Trustworthy Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06433)**  `arXiv:2611.06433`  
  _Benlei Cui, Chunyang Chai, Yiliang Zhang, Xiaoqian Xia, Chengwen Yao, Song Li, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) remain prone to visual hallucinations when correct responses depend on subtle visual details. One line of post-training methods constructs visual contrasts through image retrieval, generation, or editing to improve fine-grained perception. However, these contrasts are not built around the model's own hallucinations, making it difficult to ensure that supervision targets its actual perceptual confusions. Another line suppresses hallucinated outputs through response correction or preference learning under fixed image conditions, but leaves the design of supervision signals on the image-input side underexplored. Inspired by how humans correct misconceptions, we introduce DreamRealm, a post-training framework that explores diverse latent hallucinated responses, minimally edits original images to make them valid, and trains the model to assign each response to its supporting image. Our data construction pipeline, RealmForgePipe, mines observed and latent hallucinations from a target model and materializes them through image editing. Using hallucinations mined from Qwen3.5-9B, we construct the Realized Hallucination Dataset (RealmSet). Our training objective, RealmMatch, constructs a likelihood score matrix across image--response combinations and combines supervised learning with bidirectional contrastive losses to reinforce correct matches and suppress mismatches. Compared with the original Qwen3.5-9B model, DreamRealm improves performance across six visual perception and hallucination benchmarks, including gains of 7.26 percentage points in HallusionBench accuracy and 11.34 percentage points in MMVP pair accuracy. Together with ablation studies on data construction and training methods, these results demonstrate that hallucination assignment is a promising paradigm for improving visual perception and mitigating hallucinations.
  </details>

- **[Grounding in the Dark: Text-Only Training for Zero-Shot Video Temporal Localization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06149)**  `arXiv:2611.06149`  
  _Divyanshu Mishra, Sabine Sternig, Rakshith Shetty, Erhan Gundogdu_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Video-LLMs are typically trained on paired video-text data for temporal tasks, yet collecting precise temporal annotations and training on large-scale video datasets are both costly. We investigate whether a text-only LLM, with no vision-language pretraining, can be trained on textual video descriptions alone to perform temporal grounding and receive video only at inference time. This setting removes paired video data from training altogether and is a step toward training multimodal models without paired annotations. We generate a 134K-example corpus of textual sequences with up to 100 time steps, adapt Qwen3-8B with LoRA on CLIP or LLM2CLIP text embeddings, and evaluate on Charades-STA and QV-Highlights without training on videos from either benchmark. LLM2CLIP, whose text encoder supports longer inputs, represents detailed descriptions better than CLIP (61.54 versus 53.86 mtIoU on Temporal-TextVid) and transfers them better to video. Still, replacing text with frame embeddings at inference yields only 4.99 mtIoU on Charades-STA and 11.12 on QV-Highlights, revealing a large modality gap. To bridge it, we propose Nearest Entity Replacement (NER), a training-free mapping that represents each video frame by the normalized mean of its nearest text-entity embeddings, so that the model receives the kind of input it was trained on. NER reaches 14.45 mtIoU on Charades-STA and 20.72 on QV-Highlights, outperforming the strongest of four families of modality-gap baselines by 5.00 and 1.71 points. NER thus enables partial transfer from text to video, while more reliable grounding will likely require encoders with better-aligned text and visual representations.
  </details>

- **[DeepfakeGenome: Toward Next-Generation Deepfake Attribution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06046)**  `arXiv:2611.06046`  
  _Shunli Wang, Xinyu Zhou, Yandan Zhao, Wenbin Liang, Taiping Yao, Ke-Yue Zhang, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  In recent years, AIGC technologies are capable of generating hyper-realistic forged facial images, posing severe threats to facial security safety. To tackle these challenges, early Deepfake research primarily centered on real-fake classification. Recently, growing efforts have been devoted to the Deepfake Attribution (DFA) of generated content. Nevertheless, existing research on Deepfake detection and attribution faces saturated performance in binary classification, limited diversity in datasets and algorithms, and imperfect evaluation protocols, which severely impede practical application. To address these limitations, we propose a comprehensive deepfake detection and attribution benchmark named DeepfakeGenome (DFG). It contains 100 facial forgery algorithms and 2M images in total, achieving 4x to 100x larger than prior DFA benchmarks. We further design 4 protocols for practical evaluation, including a novel retrieval-based attribution paradigm. Unlike previous open-set evaluation metrics, the proposed retrieval metrics are more aligned with the real-world active defense situation of blacklist registration mechanisms. Based on these elaborate designs, we investigate the performance ceiling of deepfake attribution task. Over 2k experimental evaluations are conducted, and 10 insightful findings are derived. We believe this work can provide new insights into the DFA research field. The Code and Benchmark of DFG are avaliable onthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[Whose Values Are Generated? Measuring Visual Value Leakage in Multilingual Text-to-Image Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05578)**  `arXiv:2611.05578`  
  _Zucan Lyu, Binwei Yao, Rheeya Uppaal, Jing Yao, Xiaoyuan Yi, Junjie Hu_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Text-to-image (T2I) systems serve users across languages and cultural contexts. However, evaluations of cultural representation focus mainly on recognizable elements such as landmarks, clothing, and activities, overlooking the cultural values that generated images convey. To bridge this gap, we introduce VisionValueBench, a multilingual benchmark that operationalizes six of Hofstede's cultural value dimensions (e.g., individualism versus collectivism) to investigate how T2I systems frame neutral social situations through a particular cultural orientation. We find that value-neutral prompts, which admit diverse cultural interpretations, nonetheless yield images that favor particular value orientations, a phenomenon we call visual value leakage. We evaluate prompts varying in language and geographic cues across 960 social scenarios and six T2I configurations. Generated images consistently favor the same value orientations across all six dimensions, for example, equality over hierarchy (low power distance), individual autonomy over group obligation (individualism), and enjoyment over restraint (indulgence). Prompt language has limited influence on these orientations, whereas geographic cues shift some dimensions toward the associated cultural values while largely preserving the prevailing orientations. These findings reveal substantial homogeneity in the cultural values conveyed by T2I systems worldwide.
  </details>

- **[ScoreDial: Precise Control of Visual Attributes in Image Generation via Regressor Guidance from Limited Human Ratings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05004)**  `arXiv:2611.05004`  
  _Jianbo Tang, Keita Ishii, Hiroki Sakamoto, Manabu Kikuchi, Katsuma Kitamura, Chiho Semba, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Requested-value control is stricter than continuous steering: given $c$ on a human-rating scale, the generated image should attain that value from only limited ratings. We present ScoreDial, which fits a differentiable regressor $R$ to 100-479 human-rated images; once $R$ is fixed, a requested value $c$ is defined on its output scale, and learned target conditioning is paired with error-adaptive guidance that repeatedly reduces $|R(\mathbf{x})-c|$ during sampling. Across tire slip resistance, interior visual complexity, food desire-to-eat, and soap translucency, using one set of sampler settings, ScoreDial reaches requested values and achieves $R$-MAE at most 0.013 over a range of width 0.8 and Spearman $\rho=0.980$, the maximum attainable under our protocol. Because $R$ also drives sampling, we evaluate agreement with $R$ separately from matched sweeps, an independent auditor fitted to the same ratings on a backbone no deployed regressor uses, and blinded human judgments. MPGD-flow also reaches the requested values under $R$, but ScoreDial has larger auditor displacement on every attribute, the largest among all compared methods, and a stronger MPGD-flow setting gives up target agreement without closing this gap. On every attribute, removing target conditioning leaves low $R$-MAE but more than halves auditor displacement, separating precise score correction from attribute movement. Blinded rating studies with 15 raters per attribute show mean human ratings track $c$ on tire and soap, with linear $R^2=0.665$ and $R^2=0.786$, and in forced-choice comparisons of matched sweeps, raters chose ScoreDial's as the clearer progression in at least 84% of judgments against every baseline. Code:this https URL.
  </details>

- **[LiSA: Linear Stochastic Attention for Long-Range Visual Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04216)**  `arXiv:2611.04216`  
  _Sahar Dastani, Fereshteh Shakeri, Samuel Barbeau, Kunal Mahatha, Ismail Ben Ayed, Herve Lombaert, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Self-attention has become the standard for modern sequence modeling, enabling Transformers to model long-range dependencies across a wide range of modalities. Despite these advances, its quadratic cost with respect to sequence length remains a significant computational bottleneck. Recent work addresses this limitation by introducing linear variants of self-attention, namely with kernel approximations of the softmax operator. However, their first-order aggregation restricts expressivity and struggles to capture higher-order structure. We propose Linear Stochastic Attention (LiSA), a linear attention plug-in module that enables higher-order token interactions via a stochastic diffusion process based on truncated random walks. LiSA incorporates adaptive locality, favoring semantically similar neighbors to preserve fine-grained structures, while maintaining stable and efficient training. We conduct extensive experiments on classification, semantic segmentation, and object detection tasks and show improved results over state-of-the-art methods.
  </details>

- **[TailorRig: Controllable and Structured Auxiliary Skeleton Generation for Garment-Aware Rigging](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02902)**  `arXiv:2611.02902`  
  _Yajing Chen, Xin Chen, Saidan Lu, Yeqi Yuan, Weikai Chen, Changyu Hu, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Humanoid rigging for modern characters with complex garments remains challenging due to the need for dense auxiliary skeletons and precise skinning control. Existing automatic methods primarily recover rigs from geometry, providing limited artist control over auxiliary articulation and often lacking explicit semantic or kinematic-chain structure for dense garment bones, leading to instability and weight bleeding. We present TailorRig, a framework that reformulates garment-aware humanoid rigging as a structured, controllable full-rig generation problem. This formulation shifts the goal from automatic rig recovery to artist-driven rig authoring, where both body-template compatibility and auxiliary articulation are explicit design choices. TailorRig exposes two explicit axes of artist control within a unified framework: a user-specified body skeleton template that preserves body-rig compatibility, and artist-provided semantic labels that drive an autoregressive generator to synthesize requested category-specific auxiliary bone chains. To robustly assign skinning weights under dense skeletal configurations, we further introduce a hierarchical chain-to-bone formulation: vertices are first associated to a kinematic chain, and bone weights are then refined within that chain, reducing weight bleeding between spatially adjacent but semantically unrelated structures. We evaluate TailorRig on a diverse set of characters and introduce Human-Mix, a large-scale dataset of over 38,000 humanoid models curated for garment-aware rigging. Experiments show that TailorRig improves skeleton accuracy, deformation stability, controllability, and practical usability over existing methods. Project page:this https URL; code:this https URL; dataset:this https URL
  </details>

- **[Human-Centred, Risk-Constrained Evaluation of Financial Document Recognition: Evidence, Critical Errors, and Selective Automation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02775)**  `arXiv:2611.02775`  
  _Subodh Lonkar_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Financial document recognition is often assessed using character and word error rates, although these metrics do not express whether a system preserves the financial facts on which people and organisations act. This paper develops a human-centred, risk-constrained framework for evaluating financial document recognition as evidence recovery and decision support. It defines typed financial facts linked to visible source regions and separates verbatim transcription, deterministic normalization, and business interpretation. A layered quality model covers text, localization, layout, tables, fields, fact correctness, evidence grounding, uncertainty, robustness, and operational performance. Non-compensatory acceptance gates use one-sided uncertainty bounds for critical-field errors, preventing strong aggregate scores from masking failures in amounts, identifiers, signs, periods, or entity associations. Selective automation is formulated as a constrained routing problem that accounts for residual risk, human-review capacity, and review cost. The framework further specifies annotation and adjudication procedures, leakage-resistant benchmark partitions, statistical estimands, subgroup analyses, and a reproducibility card. Formal results establish the limitations of compensatory scoring, the non-zero upper risk bound after zero observed errors, and a loss sensitive decision rule for routing uncertain cases to human review. The contribution is theoretical and methodological; no new model, dataset, or empirical performance result is claimed.
  </details>

- **[Discovering Semantic Granularity in Images through Clustering of Pretrained Visual Representations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02686)**  `arXiv:2611.02686`  
  _Ying Dai_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Semantic granularity, which refers to the levels at which visual representations organize image content into meaningful entities, is fundamental to image understanding. However, whether pretrained visual representations inherently encode semantic structures at multiple granularities remains poorly understood. This paper presents a representation-driven approach for discovering semantically meaningful image regions at different granularities through clustering of pretrained visual representations without semantic supervision. Specifically, we concatenate selected feature maps extracted from a pretrained visual model and apply a singular value decomposition (SVD)-based projection to derive pixel-wise principal representation map tensor (PRMs). The resulting PRMs captures latent semantic structures embedded in pretrained visual representations, making them accessible to subsequent semantic clustering. We then introduce the semantic continuity rate (SCR), based on the silhouette principle, to quantify the clustering behavior of PRMs at different candidate granularities. Furthermore, we analyze the signal-to-noise ratio (SNR) of SCR profiles to assess the PRMs suitability for discovering semantic-like meaningful regions at multiple granularities. Our results indicate that PRMs exhibiting a higher proportion of relatively high SNR values over an initial range of cluster numbers tend to yield more semantically meaningful regions across multiple granularities. We evaluate the proposed approach on the COCO-2017 and VOC-2012 validation datasets using Hungarian-matched intersection over union (HIoU). The results demonstrate competitive segmentation performance among the compared methods, supporting the feasibility of discovering useful semantic granularities through clustering PRMs without semantic supervision.
  </details>

- **[Blueprint2CAD: Benchmarking Foundation Models on Engineering Drawing-to-CAD Render Correspondence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02433)**  `arXiv:2611.02433`  
  _Alexander Valverde, Danny Xie-Li, Zhichao Wang, Marcos V. Conde_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Engineering drawings specify mechanical parts through orthographic projections. Recognizing which part a drawing depicts underlies part search, design reuse, and digitization, yet no public benchmark measures whether vision models can do it. We introduce Blueprint2CAD, a benchmark for drawing-to-render correspondence: given an orthographic line drawing and a shaded render, usually from another viewpoint, decide whether they show the same part. Its data layer, BlueCAD-30K, applies an open drafting pipeline to 29,925 Zero-to-CAD models, adding view-aligned renders, clean and dimensioned drawings from six viewpoints, and surface-type metadata. Frozen encoders retrieve the matching part from a 10,000-model gallery with up to 65% Recall@1 when viewpoints are known but 43% when they are not, and the four encoders we test lie within six points of each other: viewpoint, not backbone, is the bottleneck. Six zero-shot vision-language models reach only 0.52-0.67 verification accuracy, with model-specific Yes/No biases that chain-of-thought shifts but does not remove; a quick LoRA fine-tuning on in-domain pairs improves verification accuracy by a few points but sharpens these biases and leaves the viewpoint failure intact.
  </details>

- **[CUFormer: Modality-Aware CT-Ultrasound Point Cloud Registration for Orthopedic Navigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01536)**  `arXiv:2611.01536`  
  _Lixing Tan, Hongye Hao, Tianrun Gu, Qing Xia, Yuting Guo, Shuai Li, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Registering intraoperative Ultrasound (US) bone-surface point clouds to preoperative Computed Tomography (CT) models can support radiation-free orthopedic navigation. Differences in imaging physics produce distinct surface sampling patterns, complicating cross-modal feature matching. Bone strongly reflects ultrasound and casts an acoustic shadow, so US captures only the probe-facing bone surface and leaves most CT points unmatched. This partial coverage makes correspondences ambiguous on near-symmetric bones. We propose CUFormer, a modality-aware registration framework. We introduce a Modality-Aware Transformer (MAT) that encodes input modality through identity embeddings and updates features through modality-specific adapters within a shared geometric matcher. We model imaging visibility and use predicted reference-side visibility to weight coarse and fine correspondences. CUFormer achieves the lowest mean registration errors among evaluated methods on two CT-US datasets, UltraBones100k and UltraBonesHip, and the CT-RGB-D dataset SpineDepth. On UltraBones100k, it reaches mean rotation error 4.53$^\circ$, translation error 3.19\,mm, and Root Mean Squared Error (RMSE) 2.54\,mm, reducing RMSE by 65.8\% relative to the best-performing baseline by this metric. These results demonstrate CUFormer's effectiveness in aligning partial bone surfaces with preoperative CT across anatomical sites.
  </details>

- **[Resolve, Clear, or Defer: Training-Free Active Prototype Triage for Ecological Detection Pipelines](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01466)**  `arXiv:2611.01466`  
  _Anirudh Potlapally, Brijesh Nanda Brunda Yogananda, Jianyang Gu, Maanav Jajodia, Luke Meyers, Wei-Lun Chao, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Ecological monitoring increasingly relies on camera traps and aerial surveys that generate large image collections. High-recall animal detectors can localize candidate animals at scale. However, they also produce many detections that still require expert review, including false positives, ambiguous cases, and animals difficult to identify at the species level. Since exhaustive review requires substantial effort, we propose training-free active prototype triage, where reviewed detections become reusable evidence to (1) Resolve supported foreground classes, (2) Clear likely background false positives, or (3) Defer insufficiently supported cases for expert review. Across camera-trap and aerial datasets, foreground recognition and false-positive rejection benefit from different pretrained representations: BioCLIP2 provides foreground evidence while DINOv3 provides background evidence, reducing erroneous foreground clearing while improving automatic foreground recovery. Deferring unsupported cases avoids the higher error rates caused by forced decisions. Compared with confidence-thresholded multiclass k-NN and uncertainty-sampling logistic regression, prototype triage improves foreground recovery while maintaining competitive or lower review burden. Acquisition goes beyond class discovery: even after classes are represented, prototype choice determines how reliably evidence propagates through the remaining detections. Our results suggest that once animal localization reaches high recall, the challenge shifts from finding animals to deciding which examples to review, how to reuse them, and when evidence does not support an automatic decision.
  </details>

- **[Where Generated Floor Plans Stop: Calibrated Sample-Level Checks Against a Built Baseline](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00063)**  `arXiv:2611.00063`  
  _Yiliang Shao_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Floor plan generators are judged mainly by Compatibility, a graph edit distance to the requested adjacencies that is near saturation and blind to room shape. Our sample-level protocol calibrates each threshold no standard states at a declared false-rejection rate on real samples and grades them with the same checks. It reports each sample at its first failure, counts samples without a verdict apart, and attributes a stop to the generator or to its representation where a control exists. We apply it, with a production layout solver's checks, to four published generators against two built corpora, RPLAN and Modified Swiss Dwellings (MSD). A squareness check set per room type refuses 5 per cent of RPLAN apartments and 33.7 per cent of House-GAN++ plans. The gap survives resolution, tracing and orientation controls. Pooled over rooms, a Wasserstein distance on squareness orders the generators nearly as the check does but separates House-GAN++ from the built corpora by far less. Taken per plan on the check's worst-room margin, it separates them about fivefold to sevenfold. Within the GAN generators, Compatibility barely predicts which plans pass. We release the checker, which reproduces the solver's first failing check on 3,998 held-out RPLAN plans.
  </details>

- **[ACSAN: All-Convolutional Self-Attention Network Integrating Convolutional Operations with Vision Transformer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35375)**  `arXiv:2610.35375`  
  _Zimian Wei, Tianqi Zhang, Hengyue Pan_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  In the past few years, Vision Transformer (ViT) has achieved competitive performance in the field of computer vision. However, there still remain some problems. For instance, ViTs rely on large-scale training data since they simply contain a large number of trainable parameters, which slows down their learning efficiency and limits their applicability in resource-constrained scenarios. In this paper, we propose a novel deep learning model, namely All-Convolutional Self-Attention Network (ACSAN), which applies convolution operations to replace fully-connected matrices multiplications in the ViT framework. To implement ACSAN, we firstly design Dynamic Convolutional Self-Attention (DCSA) as the basic module, which employs multi-scale dilated convolutions to generate Query, Key, and Value. We define spatial self-attention and channel self-attention to generate attention features respectively. The two attention features are finally merged as the output of the DCSA module. Based on DCSA, we construct the basic ACSAN block. Specifically, we use DCSA to replace the regular self-attention operations, and use convolution-based Equivalent Convolutional Fully Connected (EqConvFC) modules to replace MLPs. To evaluate the performance, we train ACSAN on CIFAR-10, CIFAR-100 and ImageNet-1k datasets from scratch. Experimental results show that ACSAN achieves good classification accuracy (the best variants achieve 93.52\% on CIFAR-10, 73.38\% on CIFAR-100, and 83.3\% on ImageNet-1k). Moreover, in comparison with standard ViT and other state-of-the-art methods, ACSAN uses fewer parameters to achieve comparable or better accuracy.
  </details>

- **[TrackTok: Object-Centric Video Tokenization with Semantically Persistent Tokens](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33857)**  `arXiv:2610.33857`  
  _Nhi Pham, Christopher Wewer, Bernt Schiele, Jonas Fischer, Jan Eric Lenssen_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  We introduce TrackTok, an efficient video tokenization framework that produces object-centric tokens, i.e., tokens that consistently encode the same semantic objects across time, even as it moves, deforms, or undergoes partial occlusion. Conventional patch-based or existing holistic tokenizers produce token identities often tied to fixed image locations. In contrast, our approach learns temporally persistent object-centric representations that bind visual features across frames into stable semantic units. This enables substantially more compact, continuous latent video representations, reducing the number of tokens required for downstream generative modeling with state-of-the-art flow models, while preserving the semantic structure needed for high-quality synthesis. Our tokenizer offers a favorable quality-efficiency trade-off. TrackTok achieves comparable class-conditional generative FVD on UCF-101 dataset at 8 times fewer latent tokens and better latency, and outperforms existing 1D tokenization baselines on frame prediction task on Kinetics-600 across low token budgets. These results suggest that object-aligned and temporally persistent tokenization is a promising direction for scalable video generation, where reducing latent token counts is critical for efficient modeling. Our code for this work is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[Robustness of Internal Signals for Hallucination Detection in Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27041)**  `arXiv:2610.27041`  
  _Allison Chen, William Yang, Salma Abdel Magid, Jonathan Williams, Olga Russakovsky, Esin Tureci_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  When given an image and a text prompt, vision-language models (VLMs) can produce seemingly sophisticated text responses. However, they are also prone to hallucinate objects in their output, which can have detrimental consequences in safety-critical applications. To detect these hallucinations, certain methods interestingly leverage internal signals from the VLM as it generates the text response; potentially, these methods use the same signals that led to a hallucination to detect it. We conduct an in-depth analysis to understand the robustness of these detection methods. First, we show that a naive baseline of a generated word's token position in the VLM text response achieves competitive performance. Further, we reveal how some detection methods rely on token position and use this heuristic as a shortcut. Second, we explore detection methods' sensitivity to language and vision modalities. Our experiments suggest that strong detection performance is often driven by detecting language-based hallucinations, and when methods rely on token position, they tend to be less visually grounded and exhibit reduced robustness when hallucinations occur earlier in a text response. Lastly, we demonstrate that different detection methods can carry complementary signals; when ensembling methods together, adding weaker methods to an ensemble of higher performing ones still offers performance improvements. Altogether, while there may be useful internal VLM signals to detect hallucinations, our investigation reveals important limitations of the current methods that try to leverage them.
  </details>

- **[A 3D-Driven Framework for Moving-Camera Perspective-to-360° Video Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25395)**  `arXiv:2610.25395`  
  _Michael Wakeham, Mrinmoy Kumar Das, Wenqi Wang, Ayush Tewari, Boqing Gong, Deepti Ghadiyaram_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Turning a moving-camera perspective video into a 360° video means generating content outside the input frame under several constraints: observations revealed over time must be preserved, unseen regions plausibly completed, and the resulting scene kept consistent as the camera viewpoint changes. Existing methods assign these different requirements to a single conditional video model, requiring them to be jointly learned from panoramic video data. We instead present a modular, 3D-driven framework that distributes these three responsibilities across specialized components, all connected through a shared scene representation. Input observations are reconstructed explicitly in 3D, while a pretrained panoramic image model completes unobserved content from a selected panoramic render. This generated content is lifted into the shared 3D scene, where it remains spatially anchored across camera viewpoints. Without requiring any additional panoramic-video training, our framework achieves state-of-the-art input-view fidelity, perceptual quality, and geometric consistency on a static-scene subset of the Argus evaluation set. These results support our decomposed, 3D-driven formulation as a promising alternative to direct video generation in this setting.
  </details>

- **[Learning Lane-Structured Conditioning Fields for Online HD Mapping](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25284)**  `arXiv:2610.25284`  
  _Andrea Bertogalli, Anthony Dell'Eva, Marcello Ceresini, Federico Pirazzoli, Lorenzo Cipelli, Filippo D'Addeo, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  High-definition maps provide critical structural information for downstream autonomous driving tasks. Recent methods increasingly rely on external priors to improve prediction accuracy and robustness. Yet, existing prior representations expose a trade-off: dense rasters are easy to integrate but ignore certain map geometric properties, while sparse vectors preserve geometric structure but require dedicated encoders and are difficult to plug into existing architectures. Moreover, external priors can be inaccurate, costly to acquire, or difficult to maintain, motivating a structural representation that can provide geometric guidance while remaining learnable and broadly applicable. We address this limitation with the Lane Field, a dense BEV-aligned geometric representation that encodes local lane structure at each spatial location and can be either predicted from onboard observations or constructed from external map priors. Building on this representation, we introduce a plug-and-play framework that enhances online HD mapping through two complementary mechanisms: BEV feature modulation, which injects lane-structured cues into latent BEV features, and deformable attention guidance, which steers query sampling toward the underlying lane geometry. We primarily evaluate our method on OpenLane-V2, showing that conditioning BEV features and guiding deformable attention with local lane geometry enables our framework to consistently improve existing methods and achieve state-of-the-art performance on lane segment perception. Project page:this https URL
  </details>

- **[Decoupled Safety Control: A Safety-Control Algorithm for Training-Free Safety Guidance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21569)**  `arXiv:2610.21569`  
  _Huilin Zhou, Ruoxi Cheng, Yuhang Wang, Yuming Liu, Minghao Sun, Ruolong Ma, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Diffusion models achieve strong performance in text-to-image generation, but they can still produce unsafe content. Training-free safety guidance offers a practical inference-time alternative that intervenes during sampling without modifying model weights. However, many existing safeguards still rely on a coupled safety-control chain. In this chain, the same unsafe condition simultaneously specifies what should be avoided, indicates the current unsafe signal, constructs the intervention, and determines when that intervention remains active. This coupling can work when unsafe factors are explicit, but becomes less reliable once harmful and benign semantics are entangled in the current generation state, often leaving residual unsafe content or unnecessarily degrading benign-generation quality or prompt fidelity. We address this problem with Decoupled Safety Control (DSC), a plug-in safety-control algorithm for training-free safety guidance. DSC reformulates training-free safety guidance as an explicit state-dependent control process, so the executed intervention is determined from the current generation state rather than inherited directly from a single unsafe condition. Experiments on standard safety benchmarks show that DSC improves representative latent- and text-space safeguards, achieving stronger unsafe-content suppression while preserving benign-generation quality and maintaining efficient inference.
  </details>

- **[PiCo: Physics-Informed Concept Probing for Geo-Foundation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20973)**  `arXiv:2610.20973`  
  _Yihang She, Andrew Blake, Jianbo Qi, Srinivasan Keshav_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Geo-foundation models (GFMs) are evaluated by downstream predictive performance. This reveals little about which physical properties their embeddings are sensitive to or which are used in predictions. This limits developers' ability to test hypotheses about model behaviour and users' ability to select models for new tasks. We propose Physics-Informed Concept Probing (PiCo), a post-hoc framework for explaining GFM predictions. PiCo identifies concept vectors in GFM embedding space using synthetic multispectral satellite observations from a 3D radiative transfer model coupled with a game engine. These vectors characterise GFM sensitivities to physical properties, informing domain-based hypotheses about downstream performance. Projecting embeddings onto concept vectors produces low-dimensional representations for quantifying concept importance in predictions. We evaluate PiCo on three GFMs with different input modalities using seven physical concepts covering forest leaf optics and canopy structure. In ground-cover estimation, RGB-only inputs reduce dry-matter sensitivity and disproportionately degrade predictions for dry vegetation and bare ground, as anticipated by PiCo's sensitivity analysis. For hierarchical forest type and tree species classification, concept representations retain 70.0--93.8% of full-embedding accuracy. Different GFMs capture physical differences between classes, such as canopy structure between broadleaf forest and cleared ground and leaf-water-content between oak and beech. Yet they differ in the strength of these contrasts and the concepts they use for classification. PiCo provides an interpretable basis for hypothesis testing and model assessment, with potential applicability beyond Earth observation wherever physical models enable controlled simulation and pretrained embeddings are accessible for probing. Code is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[PixelMoE: Scaling Pixel-Space Diffusion with LLM-Style Mixture-of-Experts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18857)**  `arXiv:2610.18857`  
  _Ruixiao Dong, Jianmin Bao, Bozhi Luan, Zijie Meng, Xin Tao, Pengfei Wan, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Pixel-space diffusion models provide an appealing alternative to latent-space diffusion models by directly modeling raw images without relying on a learned tokenizer. However, scaling pixel-space models has predominantly followed dense strategies, tightly coupling model capacity with active computation. In this work, we show that standard LLM-style Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) architectures can be effectively transferred to pixel-space diffusion. Building on this finding, we introduce PixelMoE, a pixel-space generative framework that sparsely scales model capacity with standard LLM-style MoE, without diffusion-specific routing mechanisms or auxiliary routing objectives. Specifically, PixelMoE combines standard Token-Choice (TC) routing with fine-grained routed experts for input-adaptive sparse computation, a shared expert for capturing common representations, and a bias-based load-balancing strategy. To complement sparse capacity scaling, PixelMoE further introduces Pixel-Space Hierarchical Flow Matching, which varies spatial resolution along the denoising trajectory from global structure formation to fine-grained detail refinement. At comparable active parameter counts, PixelMoE consistently outperforms its dense counterparts across model scales, demonstrating effective sparse capacity scaling in pixel space. On class-conditional ImageNet-1K, PixelMoE achieves FID scores of 1.47 at 256x256 and 1.53 at 512x512 without representation alignment or perceptual losses, comparing favorably with leading pixel-space and latent-space generative models. Code and pretrained models are available atthis https URLandthis https URL, respectively.
  </details>

- **[Building age estimation based on widely available multi-source geospatial data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16320)**  `arXiv:2610.16320`  
  _Jialu Sui, Hongsheng Zhang, Rui Liu, Jing Ling, Ruisheng Wang, Hao Wu, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Building age is fundamental for urban infrastructure safety, operation, and planning, as it directly affects structural safety, and sustainable development. Existing methods mostly rely on detailed building information such as street view, function, or materials, which are harder to obtain than geospatial data like satellite imagery and Digital Surface Models (DSMs). However, existing methods based on those geospatial data mainly rely on optical imagery and local texture features, limiting their ability to capture structural information and distinguish buildings from different periods. Moreover, the inherent temporal ordering among building age categories is often overlooked. To address these limitations, we propose the first deep-learning framework for multi-building age estimation using widely available multi-source geospatial data, including remote sensing data and their derived products, as well as building footprint information that estimates age at both pixel and instance levels within a unified pipeline. Specifically, a large pretrained backbone with fine-tuning adapters extracts representative features, and raster-derived and property-derived fusion modules support pixel-wise and instance-wise predictions. Pixel-level estimation employs a pyramid decoder with Cosine-guided Mixture-of-Experts (CMoE) to fuse multi-source raster data across scales, capturing fine-grained building age differences, while instance-level prediction uses attention pooling and Property-Conditional Adaptive Instance Normalization (PCAdaIN) to extract instance-level representations and integrate property attributes. A Temporal Ordinal Constraint Loss (TOCL) enforces the sequential ordering of predicted ages. We also construct a building age benchmark for Hong Kong to enable comprehensive evaluation. The source code and datasets are available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[We Have Been Interpreting Diffusion Transformer Backbone Behaviors Wrong](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14517)**  `arXiv:2610.14517`  
  _Benyuan Meng, Longtao Huang, Yang Liu, Liang Lin_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  During a network forward call, diffusion backbones naturally produce many intermediate activations. If we evaluate each activation against an identical downstream benchmarking protocol, we can plot an inverted-U-shaped curve as the depth profile: the activation's performance goes up first and then down as we go into deeper layers. This profile suggests there is a semantically meaningful signal within the backbone, and it is changing. This is common knowledge in the diffusion model community, which inspires many applications including the widely-adopted REPA. However, the common interpretation of this depth profile may be inaccurate and incomplete: (i) The cause of this curve has long been treated as signal granularity changing from coarse to fine, but there has been little evidence in DiT backbones. (ii) The community has been mainly studying this phenomenon in a static way, without investigating how it changes across different training setups. In this work, we show a different mechanism picture for the profile, where the prediction target (e.g., $\epsilon$, $u$, $x_0$) sits at the center. The semantically meaningful signal is only an intermediate by-product for predicting the target, while the prediction target is the real goal for the backbone. Furthermore, the choice of prediction targets as part of the training setup can predictably affect the curve's shape. With this updated interpretation, many applications of this depth profile can be better grounded and predicted. As an example, we show how to predict the near-optimal placement of REPA based on the prediction target of the training setup. The code is atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[RT-DETR-World: Transferring Rich LLM Semantics to Real-Time Open-Vocabulary Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.09502)**  `arXiv:2610.09502`  
  _Yupeng Zhang, Ziyi Zhao, Juntao Cheng, Sheng Wang, Ningnan Guo, Ruize Han, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Open-vocabulary detection (OVD) recognizes categories unseen during training through textual category queries, yet achieving strong generalization with real-time efficiency remains challenging. Beyond vocabulary scaling, zero-shot generalization may benefit from reusable visual--semantic cues learned from seen data, including attributes, actions, states, and contextual relations. Existing real-time OVD methods primarily emphasize vocabulary coverage and efficient region/query--text matching; under strict efficiency constraints, compact detectors may struggle to absorb rich instance semantics and scene context. We propose RT-DETR-World, a compact DETR-style detector that transfers the rich semantics conveyed by descriptions during training while retaining lightweight query--text matching at inference. We construct GroundingCapv2 with three levels of supervision: category names for standard OVD, object descriptions conveying instance-level semantics, and image descriptions conveying object relations and scene context. These descriptions serve only as training-time semantic supervision. To help the compact detector absorb these semantics, we propose Dual-Path Description Alignment (DDA), combining a deployment-consistent MiniLM pathway with a training-only LLM teacher. MiniLM provides query--category supervision and object-description alignment, while offline teacher features supervise matched queries and global visual representations at the object and image levels, respectively. All teacher features are precomputed, and the teacher-side modules are removed after training. We further propose Relation-Aware Negative Relaxation (RNR), which uses teacher-derived semantic similarities to relax related negatives while preserving exact positives. Experiments demonstrate competitive zero-shot accuracy and a favorable accuracy--efficiency trade-off. The code will be released.
  </details>

- **[RDGSplat: Render-Dedicated Geometry for Novel View Synthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.09173)**  `arXiv:2610.09173`  
  _Zhijie Zheng, Xinhao Xiang, Jiawei Zhang_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Recent 3D foundation models extend to novel view synthesis by carrying a Gaussian head on the representation they already use for reconstruction. However, their rendering quality lags far behind their reconstruction accuracy, because the renderer reads a geometry and a representation that are both optimized for metric reconstruction. Prior methods alleviate this by updating the backbone weights, but they thereby sacrifice the metric predictions the model was built for. To this end, we propose RDGSplat, which decodes a geometry dedicated to rendering from a frozen 3D foundation model while keeping its metric predictions exact. In particular, we devise Render-Dedicated Geometry Decoding, which duplicates the pretrained decoders and optimizes the duplicates under photometric supervision alone. Then, a Target-Pose Conditioned Adapter is introduced to adapt the representation those decoders read towards rendering, conditioned on the predicted target pose. Extensive experiments show that RDGSplat improves novel view synthesis for three feed-forward models of two architectures on four benchmarks, with every pretrained weight frozen. On RE10K, it raises WM2.0 from 20.918 to 25.149dB by training 205.5M added parameters against a frozen 1.4B backbone, and leaves its predicted depth and pose unchanged.
  </details>

- **[Autonomous Driving Research Requires a Community-Driven Data Paradigm](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.08825)**  `arXiv:2610.08825`  
  _Jinsu Yoo, Zanming Huang, Katie Z Luo, Zheda Mai, Qiyuan Wu, Bharath Hariharan, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Autonomous driving has made remarkable progress, with recent AI advances enabling commercial deployments that are reshaping urban mobility. Yet the field remains far from its universal social promise: autonomous systems that can operate robustly anywhere, anytime, for anyone. We posit that this gap is not merely a modeling problem, but a problem of the prevailing data paradigm. Current research relies heavily on a few benchmark datasets with limited spatial and scenario coverage, even though the community has collectively produced over 600 autonomous driving datasets across nearly 50 countries. However, this abundance has not translated into broad research impact: most datasets remain significantly underused due to fragmentation, limited visibility, incompatible protocols, and benchmark incentives that concentrate attention on a few dominant datasets. We therefore argue that autonomous driving research requires a collaborative, community-driven data paradigm. Such a paradigm would improve the discovery, reuse, integration, and evaluation of diverse datasets; make underexplored data easier and more rewarding to study; and lower the barrier for new contributors. We outline its key principles, illustrate an early realization, and call for collaboration across academia and industry to transform fragmented datasets into shared community infrastructure for anytime-anywhere autonomy.
  </details>

- **[TF-PRVR: Training-Free Partially Relevant Video Retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.07925)**  `arXiv:2610.07925`  
  _Giyeol Kim, Chanho Eom_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Partially Relevant Video Retrieval (PRVR) aims to retrieve untrimmed videos containing moments relevant to a given text query. Despite recent progress, existing PRVR methods suffer from two key limitations: a fixed video decomposition scheme that causes semantic dilution, and source-domain overfitting induced by task-specific training. In this paper, we propose TF-PRVR, the first training-free framework for PRVR. TF-PRVR leverages frozen vision-language features to construct video-specific hierarchical representations. It derives temporal semantic signals from frame-level features and applies frequency-based multi-scale analysis to identify adaptive temporal boundaries, producing hierarchical segments with coherent event-level semantics. Built on these segments, TF-PRVR constructs a unified multi-scale graph and propagates query relevance across temporally and semantically related nodes. A moment-aware scoring strategy then aggregates temporally aligned relevance across scales, emphasizing consistently supported moments while suppressing isolated false responses. Without task-specific training, TF-PRVR preserves the general-purpose alignment capability of pre-trained vision-language models and avoids dataset-specific overfitting. Extensive experiments demonstrate consistent performance across datasets with diverse visual and temporal characteristics, suggesting a practical direction for training-free PRVR.
  </details>

- **[Foveated Compression: Selective High-Resolution Preservation for Token-Efficient VLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.07729)**  `arXiv:2610.07729`  
  _Donghyun Han, Jangho Park, Yuseok Bae_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Visual tokens are a major source of inference cost in vision-language models, yet simple image downsampling remains a surprisingly strong compression baseline. This raises a complementary question: under a fixed token budget, where should visual fidelity be preserved? We introduce Foveated Compression, which encodes a full-resolution image once and represents it with a mixture of native- and compressed-resolution visual tokens. A behaviorally self-distilled Foveated Merger compresses local visual tokens while preserving compatibility with their native counterparts, and a lightweight Foveated Selector chooses one of nine spatial cells to retain at native resolution using exhaustive budget-matched intervention supervision. At 11.11% visual tokens, uniform Foveated Compression shows no significant paired difference from iso-token downsampling. At 20.99%, the learned selector significantly outperforms random and fixed allocation, but remains below strong whole-image resizing, showing that localized fidelity is not universally preferable. A budget-matched region-choice oracle reaches 82.73 macro accuracy versus 69.61 for the learned selector, revealing substantial headroom within the same spatial action space. Matched probing further shows that signals predicting when compression breaks the answer are substantially more accessible after language-model computation than to the lightweight prefill-free selector. These results expose complementary bottlenecks in region selection and compressed-region fidelity.
  </details>

- **[ODDR: One-Step Deshadow Diffusion via Reward Guidance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.01291)**  `arXiv:2610.01291`  
  _Junseong Shin, Kijun Kim, Minseong Kim, Dongjin Kim, Tae Hyun Kim_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Recent advances in deep learning for shadow removal have significantly enhanced image quality and realism. However, most approaches rely on real-world paired datasets, which are costly to collect and often limited in scene diversity, leading to limited generalization. To address these limitations, we propose One-step Deshadow Diffusion via Reward guidance (ODDR), a new framework that achieves efficient and high-fidelity shadow removal without relying on real-world paired supervision. Our method begins with One-step Deshadow Diffusion (ODD), a baseline model trained on synthetic shadow data for efficient one-step shadow-free reconstruction. We further adapt ODD into ODDR using ShadowReward. In contrast to traditional, annotation-heavy approaches, ShadowReward is the first reward model for shadow removal trained entirely without human annotation. It learns to mimic human perceptual judgments by ranking synthetically generated images with controlled degradations, such as texture distortion and boundary artifacts. This reward-guided fine-tuning enables ODDR to close the synthetic-to-real domain gap. Extensive experiments show that ODD achieves strong performance without relying on real-world paired supervision, and ODDR further improves the results, narrowing the gap to fully supervised methods trained on real-world paired data while maintaining higher computational efficiency as a single-step model.
  </details>

- **[PickMoment: Continuous-Time Single-Image-to-Video via Learning Deblurring and Blur-to-Video](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.01279)**  `arXiv:2610.01279`
